# Filter transactions for the last 8 weeks excluding current week
transactions_last_8_weeks = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now", "start of week")) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now", "start of week")) <= 8)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_id=transaction_id,
    week_start=DATETIME(date_time, "start of week"),
    day_of_week=DAYOFWEEK(date_time),
    is_weekend=(DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 1) | (DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 7)  # Sunday=1, Saturday=7
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = transactions_last_8_weeks.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

# Step 1: Filter transactions for stocks and calculate initial date-related attributes.
stock_transactions_base = Transactions.WHERE(ticker.ticker_type == 'stock').CALCULATE(
    original_date_time = date_time,
    transaction_week_start_date = DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'), # Determine the start of the week for each transaction
    transaction_day_name = DAYNAME(date_time) # Get the name of the day (e.g., "Monday")
)

# Step 2: Calculate how many weeks ago each transaction's week was, 
# flag weekend transactions, and filter for the last 8 weeks (excluding the current one).
relevant_transactions = stock_transactions_base.CALCULATE(
    week_start_for_txn = transaction_week_start_date, # Carry over for partitioning
    # Calculate the number of weeks between the transaction's week start and the current week's start.
    # An offset of 0 would be the current week, 1 would be last week, etc.
    weeks_ago_offset = DATEDIFF("weeks", transaction_week_start_date, DATETIME('now', 'start of week')),
    # Create a flag (1 for weekend, 0 for weekday)
    is_weekend_indicator = IFF(ISIN(transaction_day_name, ("Saturday", "Sunday")), 1, 0)
).WHERE(
    (weeks_ago_offset >= 1) & (weeks_ago_offset <= 8) # Filter for weeks with offset 1 (last week) to 8 (8 weeks ago)
)

# Step 3: Partition the filtered transactions by the start date of their week 
# and calculate total and weekend transaction counts for each week.
weekly_stock_report = relevant_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="transactions_per_week", by=(week_start_for_txn) # Group by the week's start date
).CALCULATE(
    week_starting = week_start_for_txn, # The unique identifier for the week (its start date)
    total_stock_transactions_in_week = COUNT(Transactions), # Total stock transactions in this week
    weekend_stock_transactions_in_week = SUM(Transactions.is_weekend_indicator) # Sum of weekend flags to count weekend transactions
).ORDER_BY(week_starting.DESC()) # Order from most recent week to oldest among the 8 weeks

result = weekly_stock_report

# Filter for USA customers and their transactions from last week
usa_transactions_last_week = Customers.WHERE(country == "USA").transactions_made.WHERE(
    # Last week: from 2 weeks ago Sunday to 1 week ago Saturday (inclusive)
    (DATEDIFF("days", DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-1 week"), date_time) >= 0) &
    (DATEDIFF("days", DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-1 week"), date_time) < 7)
)

# Calculate the results
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions=COUNT(usa_transactions_last_week),
    total_amount=SUM(usa_transactions_last_week.amount)

# Calculate the number of transactions and total transaction amount for US customers last week.
# "Last week" is defined as Sunday to Saturday of the previous week.
# "The week starts on Sunday" implies DATETIME('start of week') will yield a Sunday.
transactions_summary_last_week_usa = Broker.CALCULATE(
    # var_start_of_current_week is Sunday 00:00:00 of the current week.
    var_start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    # var_start_of_last_week is Sunday 00:00:00 of the previous week.
    var_start_of_last_week = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-7 days')
).CALCULATE(
    # Identify transactions made by US customers within the "last week" date range.
    # Transactions.customer.country accesses the country of the customer who made the transaction.
    # The date range is [var_start_of_last_week, var_start_of_current_week).
    last_week_usa_filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
        (customer.country == "USA") &
        (date_time >= var_start_of_last_week) &
        (date_time < var_start_of_current_week)
    )
).CALCULATE(
    # Count the number of filtered transactions.
    num_transactions = COUNT(last_week_usa_filtered_transactions),
    # Sum the transaction amounts for the filtered transactions.
    # Assuming 'amount' is non-null; SUM typically ignores nulls if any.
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(last_week_usa_filtered_transactions.amount)
)

buy_transactions_past_10_days = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "buy") & 
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 10)
)

ticker_buy_counts = buy_transactions_past_10_days.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", 
    by=ticker_id
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=Transactions.ticker.symbol,
    num_buy_transactions=COUNT(Transactions)
)

result = ticker_buy_counts.TOP_K(2, by=num_buy_transactions.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q7] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only "buy" transactions in the past 10 days,
# and calculate the ticker symbol for partitioning.
# DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) < 10 ensures we get transactions
# from 0 full days ago (today) up to 9 full days ago.
recent_buy_transactions_with_symbol = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "buy") &
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) < 10)
).CALCULATE(
    # Extract the ticker symbol from the related Ticker record.
    # 'ticker_sym' is an intermediate variable name for the symbol.
    ticker_sym = ticker.symbol
)

# Step 2: Group the filtered transactions by ticker symbol and count the number of buy transactions for each.
buy_counts_per_ticker = recent_buy_transactions_with_symbol.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", by=(ticker_sym)  # Group by the extracted ticker symbol
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = ticker_sym,  # Output field for the ticker symbol
    num_buy_transactions = COUNT(Transactions)  # Count transactions in each group
)

# Step 3: Select the top 2 ticker symbols based on the number of buy transactions, in descending order.
top_2_bought_tickers = buy_counts_per_ticker.TOP_K(
    2, by=num_buy_transactions.DESC()
)

# The final result containing the ticker symbol and number of buy transactions for the top 2.
result = top_2_bought_tickers

# Step 1: Define the time window boundaries using the Broker collection.
# 'current_month_start_date' is the first day of the current month.
# 'six_months_ago_start_date' is the first day of the month, 6 months before the current month.
time_boundaries = Broker.CALCULATE(
    current_month_start_date=DATETIME('now', 'start of month'),
    six_months_ago_start_date=DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
)

# Step 2 & 3: Filter customers who joined within the defined time window
# (join_date >= six_months_ago_start_date AND join_date < current_month_start_date)
# and create a formatted string for their join month (e.g., "2023-04").
customers_in_period = time_boundaries.Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= six_months_ago_start_date) & (join_date < current_month_start_date)
).CALCULATE(
    # Format the join month as YYYY-MM. LPAD ensures month is two digits (e.g., '04' for April).
    cust_join_year_month_str=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 4: Partition the filtered customers by their signup month string.
# This groups customers based on the month they signed up.
monthly_signups_data = customers_in_period.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_group", by=(cust_join_year_month_str)
).CALCULATE(
    # Step 5a: Calculate PMCS (Per Month Customer Signups)
    # This is the count of customers within each monthly signup group.
    month_col=cust_join_year_month_str,
    PMCS_val=COUNT(Customers),
    # Step 5b: Calculate PMAT (Per Month Average Transaction amount)
    # This is the average 'amount' of all transactions made by customers who signed up in this specific month.
    PMAT_val=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
)

# Step 6: Select the final columns with the required names and order by month.
result = monthly_signups_data.CALCULATE(
    month=month_col,
    PMCS=PMCS_val,
    PMAT=PMAT_val
).ORDER_BY(month_col.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q10] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter customers who joined in 2022 and have at least one transaction.
# These are the customers relevant for the AR calculation.
relevant_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    (YEAR(join_date) == 2022) & (HAS(transactions_made) == 1)
)

# Step 2: For these relevant customers, get their country and determine if their status is 'active'.
customer_info_for_ar = relevant_customers.CALCULATE(
    customer_country_field=country,
    is_customer_active_status=(status == 'active') # True if status is 'active', False otherwise
)

# Step 3: Partition these customers by their country.
country_partitioned_data = customer_info_for_ar.PARTITION(
    name="country_group",  # Name for the collection of groups
    by=(customer_country_field)  # Partition key
)

# Step 4: Calculate AR for each country.
# Within each partition (country_group):
# - 'Customers' refers to the subset of 'customer_info_for_ar' for that specific country.
# - COUNT(Customers) gives the total number of customers with transactions in that country (denominator).
# - SUM(IFF(Customers.is_customer_active_status == True, 1, 0)) counts active customers with transactions (numerator).
ar_by_country = country_partitioned_data.CALCULATE(
    output_country_name=customer_country_field,
    # Denominator: Total customers in this country group (who joined in 2022 and have transactions)
    total_cust_with_trans_in_country=COUNT(Customers),
    # Numerator: Active customers in this country group (who joined in 2022 and have transactions)
    active_cust_with_trans_in_country=SUM(IFF(Customers.is_customer_active_status == True, 1, 0)),
    # Calculate Activity Ratio (AR)
    # AR = (Numerator / Denominator) * 100
    # Ensure no division by zero.
    calculated_activity_ratio=IFF(
        COUNT(Customers) > 0, # Check if denominator is greater than 0
        (SUM(IFF(Customers.is_customer_active_status == True, 1, 0)) * 100.0) / COUNT(Customers),
        0.0 # Default AR to 0.0 if no customers in the group (denominator is 0)
    )
)

# Step 5: Select only the country and the calculated AR for the final result.
final_result = ar_by_country.CALCULATE(
    country=output_country_name,
    AR=calculated_activity_ratio
)

result = Tickers.CALCULATE(
    # Store the original ticker symbol
    ticker_symbol_out=symbol,
    # Filter transactions to include only 'sell' types from the past month
    sell_transactions_last_month=transactions_of.WHERE(
        (transaction_type == "sell") &
        (date_time >= DATETIME('now', '-1 month')) # Filters for transactions from one month ago up to the current time
    ),
    # Calculate total amount from these sell transactions
    total_amount_from_sells_calc=SUM(sell_transactions_last_month.amount),
    # Calculate total tax from these sell transactions
    total_tax_from_sells_calc=SUM(sell_transactions_last_month.tax),
    # Calculate total commission from these sell transactions
    total_commission_from_sells_calc=SUM(sell_transactions_last_month.commission),
    # Calculate Selling Profit Margin (SPM)
    # SPM = (Total Amount from Sells - (Tax + Commission)) / Total Amount from Sells * 100
    # Use IFF to handle cases where total_amount_from_sells_calc is 0 to prevent division by zero
    calculated_spm_value=IFF(
        total_amount_from_sells_calc == 0,
        0.0,  # If no sales amount, SPM is 0
        (total_amount_from_sells_calc - (total_tax_from_sells_calc + total_commission_from_sells_calc)) / total_amount_from_sells_calc * 100.0
    )
).WHERE(
    # Only include tickers that had at least one 'sell' transaction in the past month
    HAS(transactions_of.WHERE(
        (transaction_type == "sell") &
        (date_time >= DATETIME('now', '-1 month'))
    )) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    # Select and rename the final fields for the output
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_out,
    spm=calculated_spm_value
)

# Step 1: Prepare price data with month and ticker symbol
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    month = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0")),
    symbol = ticker.symbol
)

# Step 2: Group by symbol and month to calculate monthly statistics
monthly_stats = price_info.PARTITION(name="monthly_groups", by=(symbol, month)).CALCULATE(
    symbol = symbol,
    month = month,
    avg_close = AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    highest_price = MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    lowest_price = MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Calculate month-over-month change using PREV function
result = monthly_stats.CALCULATE(
    symbol = symbol,
    month = month,
    avg_close = avg_close,
    highest_price = highest_price,
    lowest_price = lowest_price,
    MoMC = (avg_close - PREV(avg_close, by=(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()), per="monthly_groups")) / 
           PREV(avg_close, by=(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()), per="monthly_groups")
).ORDER_BY(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC())

# Step 1: Prepare data from DailyPrices, extracting year, month, symbol, and relevant price fields.
price_info_for_agg = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    calc_year = YEAR(date),
    calc_month = MONTH(date),
    calc_symbol = ticker.symbol  # Get the ticker symbol directly
    # Original price fields (close, high, low) will be accessed via DailyPrices collection in the aggregation step
)

# Step 2: Partition by symbol, year, and month to calculate monthly aggregates.
# The result, 'monthly_stats', will have one row per unique symbol-year-month combination.
monthly_stats = price_info_for_agg.PARTITION(name="monthly_group", by=(calc_symbol, calc_year, calc_month)).CALCULATE(
    stat_symbol = calc_symbol,    # Field from the 'by' clause of the partition
    stat_year = calc_year,      # Field from the 'by' clause of the partition
    stat_month = calc_month,    # Field from the 'by' clause of the partition
    avg_close = AVG(DailyPrices.close),  # Aggregate over the 'DailyPrices' data within the current partition group
    max_high = MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low = MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Partition the 'monthly_stats' by ticker symbol to prepare for MoMC calculation.
# This creates a context where 'PREV' can operate per ticker.
# 'ticker_series' is the name of this partition group.
ticker_series_partition = monthly_stats.PARTITION(name="ticker_series", by=(stat_symbol))

# Step 4: Calculate MoMC using the PREV window function.
# This operates on the 'monthly_stats' sub-collection within each 'ticker_series' partition group.
data_with_momc = ticker_series_partition.monthly_stats.CALCULATE(
    # Carry forward fields from 'monthly_stats' for the final output
    final_symbol = stat_symbol,
    final_year = stat_year,
    final_month = stat_month,
    final_avg_close = avg_close,
    final_max_high = max_high,
    final_min_low = min_low,

    # Get the average closing price of the previous month for the same ticker
    # PREV is ordered by year and month, and scoped per "ticker_series" (i.e., per ticker symbol)
    prev_avg_c = PREV(avg_close, by=(stat_year.ASC(), stat_month.ASC()), per="ticker_series", default=None),

    # Calculate MoMC: (current_avg_close - prev_avg_close) / prev_avg_close
    # If prev_avg_c is None (no previous month), MoMC is None.
    momc_val = IFF(
        prev_avg_c != None,
        (avg_close - prev_avg_c) / prev_avg_c,
        None
    )
)

# Step 5: Select and format the final output fields, and order the results.
# The month is formatted as 'YYYY-MM'.
result = data_with_momc.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = final_symbol,
    month = JOIN_STRINGS("-", final_year, LPAD(final_month, 2, "0")), # LPAD ensures month is two digits (e.g., '01' for January)
    average_closing_price = final_avg_close,
    highest_price = final_max_high,
    lowest_price = final_min_low,
    MoMC = momc_val
).ORDER_BY(final_symbol.ASC(), final_year.ASC(), final_month.ASC()) # Order chronologically per ticker

# Calculate join month/year for each customer and count transactions in that month
customer_join_month_transactions = Customers.CALCULATE(
    customer_id=_id,
    customer_name=name,
    join_year=YEAR(join_date),
    join_month=MONTH(join_date),
    transactions_in_join_month=COUNT(
        transactions_made.WHERE(
            (YEAR(date_time) == YEAR(join_date)) & 
            (MONTH(date_time) == MONTH(join_date))
        )
    )
).WHERE(transactions_in_join_month > 0)

# Find the customer with the highest number of transactions in their join month

    customer_id=customer_id,
    customer_name=customer_name,
    num_transactions=transactions_in_join_month
)

    # Store original _id and name in intermediate fields
    cust_id=_id,
    cust_name=name,
    # Determine the year and month the customer joined
    signup_year_val=YEAR(join_date),
    signup_month_val=MONTH(join_date),
    # Count transactions made by the customer in their signup month and year
    # YEAR(date_time) and MONTH(date_time) refer to the transaction's date_time
    # signup_year_val and signup_month_val refer to the customer's join date components
    num_transactions_in_signup_month=COUNT(transactions_made.WHERE(
        (YEAR(date_time) == signup_year_val) & (MONTH(date_time) == signup_month_val)
    ))
).TOP_K(
    1,  # Select the top 1 customer
    by=num_transactions_in_signup_month.DESC()  # Order by the count of transactions in descending order
).CALCULATE(
    # Select the required fields for the final output
    _id=cust_id,
    name=cust_name,
    number_of_transactions=num_transactions_in_signup_month

# Filter transactions to the specified date range (Jan 1, 2023 to Mar 31, 2023)
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (date_time >= "2023-01-01") & (date_time <= "2023-03-31")
)

# Group by transaction type and calculate metrics
transaction_type_stats = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="transaction_types", 
    by=transaction_type
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,
    distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),
    avg_shares=AVG(Transactions.shares)
)

# Get top 3 transaction types by number of distinct customers
result = transaction_type_stats.TOP_K(3, by=distinct_customers.DESC())

# Step 1: Filter transactions to the specified date range (inclusive)
transactions_in_range = Transactions.WHERE(
    (date_time >= "2023-01-01T00:00:00.000") & (date_time <= "2023-03-31T23:59:59.999")
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by transaction_type
# Step 3: Calculate the number of distinct customers and average shares for each type
# The fields transaction_type, customer_id, and shares are from the Transactions collection.
# Within the CALCULATE clause, transaction_type refers to the partition key.
# NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id) and AVG(Transactions.shares) compute aggregates
# for the transactions within the current partition group (i.e., for the specific transaction_type
# and within the filtered date range).
stats_by_type = transactions_in_range.PARTITION(name="tx_type_groups", by=(transaction_type)).CALCULATE(
    tx_type_val=transaction_type,
    num_distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),
    avg_shares_val=AVG(Transactions.shares)
)

# Step 4: Select the top 3 transaction types by the number of distinct customers
# The CALCULATE in the previous step has already defined the required output fields.
top_3_transaction_types = stats_by_type.TOP_K(3, by=num_distinct_customers.DESC())

# Assign to final result variable
result = top_3_transaction_types

[DEBUG] [Q26] Code generated by gemini:
# Filter transactions to the last 30 days (inclusive)
recent_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30) & (date_time <= DATETIME('now'))
)

# Add customer country and transaction amount to the filtered transactions
# customer.country gives the country of the customer who made the transaction
transactions_with_country_details = recent_transactions.CALCULATE(
    cust_country = customer.country,
    transaction_value = amount
)

# Partition by customer country
country_grouped_transactions = transactions_with_country_details.PARTITION(
    name="country_transactions_group",  # Name for the partition group
    by=(cust_country)
)

# Calculate aggregates for each country: number of transactions and total transaction amount
country_aggregates = country_grouped_transactions.CALCULATE(
    country_name = cust_country,  # The country name is the partition key
    num_of_transactions = COUNT(transactions_with_country_details),  # Count transactions within this country's partition
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(transactions_with_country_details.transaction_value)  # Sum transaction amounts within this country's partition
)

# Get the top 5 countries by total transaction amount
top_countries_by_transaction_amount = country_aggregates.TOP_K(
    5,
    by=total_transaction_amount.DESC()
)

result = top_countries_by_transaction_amount

# Step 1: Filter transactions for status 'success' and Q1 2023, and extract the transaction month.
# Q1 2023 includes months 1 (January), 2 (February), and 3 (March).
filtered_transactions_with_month = Transactions.WHERE(
    (status == 'success') &
    (YEAR(date_time) == 2023) &
    (MONTH(date_time) >= 1) & (MONTH(date_time) <= 3)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month = MONTH(date_time)
    # The 'price' field is an original field of Transactions and will be available for aggregation.
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by month and calculate the average transaction price for each month.
# The result is ordered by month in ascending order.
monthly_avg_transaction_price = filtered_transactions_with_month.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_grouped_transactions", by=(transaction_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month = transaction_month,  # Output the month
    average_price = AVG(Transactions.price)  # Calculate the average price for transactions in this month group
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

result = monthly_avg_transaction_price

high_value_sales_payments = Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", payment_date, DATETIME("now")) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", payment_date, DATETIME("now")) <= 8)
).CALCULATE(

    is_weekend=(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) == 1) | (DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) == 7)
)

    week=week_start,
    total_payments=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by gemini:
# Define date boundaries for the analysis period.
# 'analysis_start_dt' is the start of the week, 8 weeks prior to the current week's start.
# 'analysis_end_dt' is the end of the last full week (i.e., one day before the current week's start).
date_boundaries = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    # Using DATETIME('now', 'start of week') to get the start of the current week.
    # Then, subtract 8 weeks to find the start of the 8-week-ago period.
    analysis_start_dt = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-8 weeks'),
    # End of the analysis period is one day before the start of the current week.
    analysis_end_dt = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-1 day')
)

# 1. Filter sales with sale_price > $30,000.
# 2. Access their associated payments. The date_boundaries are available from the context.
payments_from_high_value_sales = date_boundaries.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000.0).payment

# 3. Filter these payments to include only those within the last 8 full calendar weeks.
# 4. For each relevant payment, determine its week's start date and if it occurred on a weekend.
payments_in_analysis_period = payments_from_high_value_sales.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= analysis_start_dt) & (payment_date <= analysis_end_dt)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_for_payment = DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_payment_on_weekend = ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# 5. Group the filtered payments by their week's start date.
#    For each week, calculate the total number of payments and the number of weekend payments.
weekly_payment_stats = payments_in_analysis_period.PARTITION(
    name="payments_grouped_by_week", by=(week_start_for_payment)
).CALCULATE(
    week_iso_date = week_start_for_payment,  # The partition key, representing the start of the week.
    total_payments_in_week = COUNT(payments_in_analysis_period), # Total payments in this week's group.
    weekend_payments_in_week = SUM(IFF(payments_in_analysis_period.is_payment_on_weekend, 1, 0)) # Sum of weekend payments (1 if true, 0 if false).
).ORDER_BY(week_iso_date.ASC()) # 6. Order results by week in ascending order.

result = weekly_payment_stats

# First, get cars with their most recent inventory status
cars_with_latest_inventory = Cars.WHERE(
    HAS(sale_records) == 1 & HAS(inventory_snapshots) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=make,
    car_model=model,
    latest_inventory_status=inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC()) == 1
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory,
    max_sale_price=MAX(sale_records.sale_price)
)

# Filter for cars no longer in inventory and group by make/model
result = cars_with_latest_inventory.WHERE(
    latest_inventory_status == False
).PARTITION(name="make_model_groups", by=(car_make, car_model)).CALCULATE(
    make=car_make,
    model=car_model,
    highest_sale_price=MAX(Cars.max_sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

# Step 1: Start from the Sales collection. For each sale, retrieve information about the car,
# its make, model, and determine its latest inventory status.
sales_with_car_details = Sales.CALCULATE(
    # Store the sale price for later aggregation.
    sale_price_val=sale_price,
    # Get the make and model of the car involved in the sale.
    car_make_val=car.make,
    car_model_val=car.model,
    # Determine if the car is no longer in inventory based on its latest snapshot.
    # Access the car's inventory_snapshots.
    # RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), allow_ties=True) == 1 finds the latest snapshot(s) for this specific car.
    # .SINGULAR() selects one if multiple (e.g., same date, though is_in_inventory should be consistent).
    # Check if its is_in_inventory status is False.
    is_car_no_longer_in_inventory=(car.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), allow_ties=True) == 1
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory == False)
)

# Step 2: Filter these sales records to include only those where the car
# is confirmed to be no longer in inventory.
# Cars in the Sales collection are inherently "sold".
filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory = sales_with_car_details.WHERE(
    sales_with_car_details.is_car_no_longer_in_inventory == True
)

# Step 3: Group these filtered sales by car make and model.
# For each group, calculate the maximum sale price.
# The collection being partitioned is `filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory`.
# `MAX(filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.sale_price_val)` will compute the max sale_price_val
# from the sales records within each (make, model) group.
max_sale_prices_by_make_model = filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.PARTITION(
    name="make_model_group", by=(car_make_val, car_model_val)
).CALCULATE(
    # Output make and model from the partition keys.
    output_make=car_make_val,
    output_model=car_model_val,
    # Calculate the highest sale price for this group.
    output_highest_sale_price=MAX(filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.sale_price_val)
)

# Step 4: Select the final required fields (make, model, highest_sale_price)
# and order the results by the highest sale price in descending order.
result = max_sale_prices_by_make_model.CALCULATE(
    make=output_make,
    model=output_model,
    highest_sale_price=output_highest_sale_price
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

# Filter salespersons hired between 2022-2023 (inclusive)
eligible_salespersons = Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
)

# Get sales from these salespersons in the last 6 months excluding current month
# Using DATETIME to get the date range
sales_in_period = eligible_salespersons.sales_made.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME("now")) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME("now")) <= 6)
).CALCULATE(
    sale_month = DATETIME(sale_date, "start of month"),
    sale_price = sale_price,
    salesperson_id = salesperson_id
)

# Group by month and calculate metrics
monthly_metrics = sales_in_period.PARTITION(name="months", by=sale_month).CALCULATE(
    month = sale_month,
    pmsps = COUNT(Sales),  # Per month salesperson sales count
    pmsr = SUM(Sales.sale_price)  # Per month sales revenue
)

# To ensure all 6 months are included (even with 0 values), we need to create a complete month range
# and left join with our results
result = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q39] Code generated by gemini:
# Define Date Boundaries for the reporting period (last 6 full months, excluding current month)
current_month_start_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
# period_start_dt will be the first day of the month, 6 months ago.
# e.g., if current_month_start_dt is July 1st, period_start_dt will be January 1st.
period_start_dt = DATETIME(current_month_start_dt, '-6 months')
# period_end_exclusive_dt will be the first day of the current month.
# Sales up to, but not including, this date will be considered.
period_end_exclusive_dt = current_month_start_dt

# Step 1: Create a scaffold of all unique months within the desired reporting period.
# This is based on all sales in the system to ensure all calendar months with any sale are represented.
all_sales_with_month_info = Dealership.Sales.CALCULATE(
    sale_month_val = DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') # Truncate sale_date to the start of its month
)

# Get distinct months from all sales and filter them to the 6-month reporting window.
# These distinct months will form the rows of our report.
distinct_months_for_report = all_sales_with_month_info.PARTITION(
    name="month_partition", by=(sale_month_val) # Group by the calculated sale_month_val to get distinct months
).CALCULATE(
    report_month_value = sale_month_val # Select the distinct month value
).WHERE(
    (report_month_value >= period_start_dt) & (report_month_value < period_end_exclusive_dt) # Filter to the defined 6-month period
)

# Step 2: For each month in our scaffold, calculate PMSPS and PMSR.
# Metrics are specific to salespersons hired between 2022 and 2023.
monthly_metrics = distinct_months_for_report.CALCULATE(
    # The month being reported on
    Month = report_month_value,

    # PMSPS: Per Month Salesperson Sales count
    # Count sales made by salespersons hired in 2022-2023, where the sale_date (truncated to month) matches report_month_value.
    PMSPS = COUNT(
        Dealership.Salespersons.WHERE(
            (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023) # Filter salespersons by hire year
        ).sales_made.WHERE(
            DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') == report_month_value # Filter their sales to the specific month from the scaffold
        )
    ),

    # PMSR: Per Month Sales Revenue
    # Sum of sale_price for the same set of filtered sales.
    PMSR = DEFAULT_TO( # Use DEFAULT_TO to ensure 0.0 if there are no sales, as SUM might return null.
        SUM(
            Dealership.Salespersons.WHERE(
                (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023) # Filter salespersons by hire year
            ).sales_made.WHERE(
                DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') == report_month_value # Filter their sales to the specific month
            ).sale_price # Access the sale_price for summation
        ),
        0.0 # Default to 0.0 if the sum is null (i.e., no sales in that month for these salespersons)
    )
)

# Step 3: Order the results by month in ascending order.
final_report = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(Month.ASC())

    ) / SUM(Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).car.cost) * 100
)

# Calculate intermediate values for total revenue and total cost for sales in 2023
gpm_intermediate_calc = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    total_revenue_2023 = SUM(Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).sale_price),
    total_cost_2023 = SUM(Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).car.cost)
)

# Calculate the final GPM using the intermediate values
gpm_for_2023_car_sales = gpm_intermediate_calc.CALCULATE(
    gpm = IFF(total_cost_2023 > 0, (total_revenue_2023 - total_cost_2023) / total_cost_2023 * 100.0, 0.0)
)

# Step 1: Extract month/year info and group payments by month
payment_info = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    year=YEAR(payment_date),
    month=MONTH(payment_date),
    year_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(payment_date), LPAD(MONTH(payment_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 2: Group by year-month and calculate total payments per month
monthly_payments = payment_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=(year, month)).CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    total_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoM change using PREV function
result = monthly_payments.CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    total_payments=total_payments,
    prev_month_payments=PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0),
    mom_change=total_payments - PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0)
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC(), month.ASC())

result = Salespersons.CALCULATE(
    # Keep original names for later use, aliasing them to avoid conflicts
    sp_first_name=first_name,
    sp_last_name=last_name,
    # Calculate the number of sales in the last 30 days
    num_sales_last_30_days=COUNT(sales_made.WHERE(DATEDIFF("days", sale_date, DATETIME('now')) <= 30)),
    # Calculate the total revenue from sales in the last 30 days
    # If SUM on an empty set returns NULL, DEFAULT_TO ensures 0.0.
    # However, the subsequent WHERE clause makes this less critical here.
    revenue_last_30_days=DEFAULT_TO(SUM(sales_made.WHERE(DATEDIFF("days", sale_date, DATETIME('now')) <= 30).sale_price), 0.0)
).WHERE(
    # Filter to include only salespersons with at least one sale in the period
    num_sales_last_30_days > 0
).TOP_K(
    5,  # Get the top 5
    by=num_sales_last_30_days.DESC()  # Order by the number of sales in descending order
).CALCULATE(
    # Select and rename fields for the final output
    first_name=sp_first_name,
    last_name=sp_last_name,
    total_sales_count=num_sales_last_30_days,
    total_revenue_amount=revenue_last_30_days
)

[DEBUG] [Q53] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Augment Sales data with customer's state, sale price, and customer ID.
# We access customer.state via the relationship from Sales to Customers.
sales_with_customer_state = Sales.CALCULATE(
    customer_state_attr=customer.state,
    sale_price_attr=sale_price,
    customer_id_attr=customer_id
)

# Step 2: Partition the augmented sales data by customer_state_attr.
# This groups sales by the state of the customer.
partitioned_by_state = sales_with_customer_state.PARTITION(
    name="sales_per_state",
    by=(customer_state_attr)
)

# Step 3: Calculate total revenue and number of unique customers for each state.
# SUM(sales_with_customer_state.sale_price_attr) sums the sale prices for sales within the current state partition.
# NDISTINCT(sales_with_customer_state.customer_id_attr) counts unique customers for sales within the current state partition.
state_revenue_and_customers = partitioned_by_state.CALCULATE(
    state=customer_state_attr,
    total_revenue=SUM(sales_with_customer_state.sale_price_attr),
    num_unique_customers=NDISTINCT(sales_with_customer_state.customer_id_attr)
)

# Step 4: Select the top 5 states by total_revenue.
top_5_states_by_revenue = state_revenue_and_customers.TOP_K(
    5,
    by=total_revenue.DESC()
)

previous_week_payments = PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    # Get payments from the previous ISO week
    (DATEDIFF("days", DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-7 days"), payment_date) >= 0) &
    (DATEDIFF("days", DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-7 days"), payment_date) < 7)
).CALCULATE(

    payment_method=payment_method,
    payment_amount=payment_amount

    name="daily_method_groups", 
    by=(payment_date, payment_method)

# Define the start and end dates for the previous ISO week.
# Assumes DATETIME('now', 'start of week') correctly identifies Monday as the start of an ISO week.
start_of_current_week_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_previous_week_dt = DATETIME(start_of_current_week_dt, '-7 days')
end_of_previous_week_dt = DATETIME(start_of_current_week_dt, '-1 day') # This will be Sunday of the previous week

# Filter payments that occurred within the calculated previous ISO week.
payments_in_previous_week = PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_previous_week_dt) & (payment_date <= end_of_previous_week_dt)
)

# Group the filtered payments by payment_date and payment_method.
# Then, calculate the sum of payment_amount for each group.
# The fields 'payment_date' and 'payment_method' from the PARTITION's 'by' clause
# are used to define 'calc_payment_date' and 'calc_payment_method'.
# SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount) aggregates amounts for records within each partition group.
daily_payments_summary = payments_in_previous_week.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_group", by=(payment_date, payment_method)
).CALCULATE(
    calc_payment_date=payment_date,
    calc_payment_method=payment_method,
    calc_total_amount=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Select the final fields for the output and order the results.
# The field names are chosen to match common expectations for such a report.
result = daily_payments_summary.CALCULATE(
    payment_date=calc_payment_date,
    payment_method=calc_payment_method,
    total_amount=calc_total_amount
).ORDER_BY(payment_date.ASC(), payment_method.ASC())

sales_2023_info = Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer.state,

[DEBUG] [Q61] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter sales for 2023 and calculate initial necessary fields.
sales_filtered = Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).CALCULATE(
    c_state=customer.state,
    s_price_val=sale_price,
    s_date_month=MONTH(sale_date),
    s_date_year_start=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of year') # First day of the year of the sale
)

# Step 2: Calculate the quarter start date using the fields from the previous step.
sales_info = sales_filtered.CALCULATE(
    c_state=c_state, # Pass through customer state
    s_price_val=s_price_val, # Pass through sale price
    # Determine the first date of the quarter based on the sale month
    q_start_date=IFF(
        s_date_month <= 3, s_date_year_start, # Q1
        IFF(
            s_date_month <= 6, DATETIME(s_date_year_start, '+3 months'), # Q2
            IFF(
                s_date_month <= 9, DATETIME(s_date_year_start, '+6 months'), # Q3
                DATETIME(s_date_year_start, '+9 months') # Q4
            )
        )
    )
)

# Step 3: Partition by customer state and quarter start date, then calculate total sales for each group.
quarterly_sales_by_state_result = sales_info.PARTITION(
    name="state_quarter_group",  # Name for the partition context
    by=(c_state, q_start_date)   # Partition keys: customer state and quarter start date
).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=c_state,      # Output field: customer's state (from partition key)
    quarter_start_date=q_start_date, # Output field: quarter start date (from partition key)
    total_sales=SUM(sales_info.s_price_val) # Output field: sum of sale prices for the group
)

# Assign the final result to the 'result' variable.
result = quarterly_sales_by_state_result

    (start_dt >= DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of month'), '-3 months')) &
    (start_dt < DATETIME('now', 'start of month'))
).CALCULATE(
    p_id = patient_id,  # Alias for patient_id for clarity in aggregation
    treatment_month_year_str = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(start_dt), LPAD(MONTH(start_dt), 2, "0")), # Format as YYYY-MM
    is_biologic_treatment_flag = (drug.drug_type == 'biologic') # Flag for biologic drug treatments
)

# Step 2: Partition the augmented treatments by the 'treatment_month_year_str'.
# Then, for each month, calculate the distinct count of all patients and
# the distinct count of patients who received biologic drug treatments.
# The results are ordered by month.
result = treatments_info.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_treatments_group", by=(treatment_month_year_str)  # Group by the YYYY-MM string
).CALCULATE(
    month_label = treatment_month_year_str,  # The month (YYYY-MM)
    patient_count = NDISTINCT(monthly_treatments_group.p_id),  # Distinct patients in this month
    biologic_treatment_patient_count = NDISTINCT(
        monthly_treatments_group.WHERE(is_biologic_treatment_flag == True).p_id  # Distinct patients with biologic treatments in this month
    )
).ORDER_BY(month_label.ASC())

# Get treatments from the last 12 months (excluding current month)
treatment_info = Treatments.CALCULATE(
    treatment_month = DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month')
).WHERE(
    # Filter for last 12 months, excluding current month
    (DATEDIFF("months", treatment_month, DATETIME('now', 'start of month')) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", treatment_month, DATETIME('now', 'start of month')) <= 12)
)

# Group by month and calculate PMPD and PMTC

    month = treatment_month,
    PMPD = NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient_id),  # Per Month Patient Diagnoses
    PMTC = COUNT(Treatments)                  # Per Month Treatment Count
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Step 1: Define date boundaries at the top level (DermTreatment graph)
date_boundaries = DermTreatment.CALCULATE(
    current_month_start_val=DATETIME('now', 'start of month'),
    twelve_months_ago_val=DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of month'), '-12 months')
)

# Step 2: Filter Treatments to the last 12 months (excluding current) and prepare a month key
# The 'start_dt' field from Treatments is used for filtering.
# 'patient_id' is an existing field in Treatments.
# A new field 'treatment_month_key' is calculated for partitioning.
treatments_filtered_and_prepared = date_boundaries.Treatments.WHERE(
    (start_dt >= twelve_months_ago_val) & (start_dt < current_month_start_val)
).CALCULATE(
    treatment_month_key=DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month')
    # patient_id is implicitly available from the Treatments collection
)

# Step 3: Partition the filtered treatments by the calculated month key
# Step 4: Calculate PMPD and PMTC for each month
# 'treatment_month_key' is the partition key and is directly usable in CALCULATE.
# 'Treatments.patient_id' refers to the patient_id field of the Treatments records within the current partition group.
# 'Treatments' in COUNT(Treatments) refers to the Treatments records within the current partition group.
monthly_aggregates = treatments_filtered_and_prepared.PARTITION(name="monthly_data", by=(treatment_month_key)).CALCULATE(
    month_col=treatment_month_key,
    PMPD_val=NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient_id),
    PMTC_val=COUNT(Treatments)
)

# Step 5: Select the final columns and order the results by month in ascending order
result = monthly_aggregates.CALCULATE(
    month=month_col,
    PMPD=PMPD_val,
    PMTC=PMTC_val
).ORDER_BY(month_col.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q74] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: For each patient with treatments, determine the year of their first treatment.
# This creates a collection where each row corresponds to a patient and includes the year of their first treatment.
patient_first_treatment_dates = Patients.WHERE(HAS(treatments_received) == 1).CALCULATE(
    # patient_id is implicitly part of each record from Patients.
    # We calculate first_treatment_year_calc for each patient based on the earliest treatment start date.
    first_treatment_year_calc = YEAR(MIN(treatments_received.start_dt))
)

# Step 2: Group patients by the year of their first treatment.
# Count the number of new patients for each year and order by year.
yearly_new_patient_counts = patient_first_treatment_dates.PARTITION(
    name="patients_by_first_treatment_year", by=(first_treatment_year_calc)
).CALCULATE(
    year_val = first_treatment_year_calc,  # The year of first treatment.
    # COUNT(Patients) counts the number of original Patient records that fall into this partition group
    # (i.e., patients whose first treatment was in year_val).
    num_new_patients_for_year = COUNT(Patients)
).ORDER_BY(year_val.ASC()) # Order by year is essential for the PREV function to work correctly.

# Step 3: Calculate the New Patients Increase (NPI) for each year.
# NPI is the difference between the current year's new patients and the previous year's new patients.
npi_results = yearly_new_patient_counts.CALCULATE(
    year = year_val,  # Final column name as requested: 'year'.
    number_of_new_patients = num_new_patients_for_year,  # Final column name as requested: 'number_of_new_patients'.
    # The PREV function looks up the value of num_new_patients_for_year from the previous row,
    # based on the ascending order of year_val.
    # 'default=0' handles the first year in the series, where there's no preceding year.
    NPI = num_new_patients_for_year - PREV(num_new_patients_for_year, by=year_val.ASC(), default=0)  # Final column name as requested: 'NPI'.
)

# Step 1: Calculate the global average PASI scores for day 7 and day 100.
# This calculation is performed on the 'Outcomes' collection, filtered to include
# only records where both 'day7_pasi_score' and 'day100_pasi_score' are not null.
pasi_averages = DermTreatment.CALCULATE(
    avg_d7_score = AVG(Outcomes.WHERE((day7_pasi_score != None) & (day100_pasi_score != None)).day7_pasi_score),
    avg_d100_score = AVG(Outcomes.WHERE((day7_pasi_score != None) & (day100_pasi_score != None)).day100_pasi_score)
)

# Step 2: Use the calculated averages to compute the D7D100PIR.
# The result will be a single row with the D7D100PIR percentage.
# Multiplying by 100.0 ensures the result is a float representing a percentage.
result = pasi_averages.CALCULATE(
    d7d100pir_percentage = (avg_d100_score - avg_d7_score) / avg_d7_score * 100.0
)

# Step 1: Filter treatments that ended in 2022 and extract the insurance type from the associated patient.
# We create an intermediate field 'treatment_ins_type' for partitioning.
treatments_for_partition = DermTreatment.Treatments.WHERE(YEAR(end_dt) == 2022).CALCULATE(
    treatment_ins_type = patient.ins_type
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered treatments by insurance type.
# For each insurance type, calculate:
#   - The average day 100 PASI score. This is done by averaging 'day100_pasi_score'
#     from the 'outcome_records' of the treatments within each insurance type group.
#   - The number of distinct patients who received these treatments. This is done by
#     counting distinct 'patient_id' from the 'patient' associated with treatments in each group.
insurance_type_summary = treatments_for_partition.PARTITION(name="ins_type_groups", by=(treatment_ins_type)).CALCULATE(
    insurance_type = treatment_ins_type,
    average_pasi_score_day_100 = AVG(Treatments.outcome_records.day100_pasi_score),
    num_distinct_patients = NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient.patient_id)
)

# Step 3: Select the top 5 insurance types.
# The sorting is by the calculated 'average_pasi_score_day_100' in ascending order (lowest first).
final_result = insurance_type_summary.TOP_K(5, by=average_pasi_score_day_100.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q86] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the cutoff date: treatments started on or after the first day of the month, 6 months ago.
cutoff_date = DATETIME('now', '-6 months', 'start of month')

# Step 1: Filter treatments by start date and make doctor's specialty available.
# We need 'doctor.speciality' for partitioning and 'tot_drug_amt' for summing.
treatments_filtered_with_specialty = Treatments.WHERE(start_dt >= cutoff_date).CALCULATE(
    treatment_doc_specialty = doctor.speciality
    # 'tot_drug_amt' is an original field of Treatments and will be accessed in the SUM.
)

# Step 2: Partition by doctor's specialty and calculate aggregates.
# For each specialty, count the number of treatments and sum the total drug amount.
specialty_summary = treatments_filtered_with_specialty.PARTITION(name="spec_partition", by=(treatment_doc_specialty)).CALCULATE(
    output_specialty = treatment_doc_specialty,  # The specialty name (partition key)
    output_num_treatments = COUNT(Treatments),    # Count of Treatments records fitting the initial filter and this partition
    output_total_drug_amount = SUM(Treatments.tot_drug_amt)  # Sum of 'tot_drug_amt' for these Treatments
)

# Step 3: Select the top 3 specialties based on the total drug amount.
# Then, format the output fields as requested.
top_3_specialties_by_drug_amount = specialty_summary.TOP_K(3, by=output_total_drug_amount.DESC()).CALCULATE(
    specialty = output_specialty,
    num_treatments = output_num_treatments,
    total_drug_amount = output_total_drug_amount
)

result = top_3_specialties_by_drug_amount

# Define the date range for the last 6 months
# six_months_ago represents the date 6 months before the current date.
six_months_ago = DATETIME('now', '-6 months')
# today represents the current date.
current_date = DATETIME('now')

# Filter the Treatments collection based on multiple criteria
filtered_treatments = Treatments.WHERE(
    # Condition 1: The diagnosis name associated with the treatment contains 'psoriasis' (case-insensitive).
    # LIKE(LOWER(diagnosis.diag_name), '%psoriasis%') checks if the lowercase version of diag_name contains 'psoriasis'.
    (LIKE(LOWER(diagnosis.diag_name), '%psoriasis%')) &

    # Condition 2: The drug used in the treatment has an FDA approval date.
    # HAS(drug.fda_appr_dt) == 1 checks if the fda_appr_dt field for the drug is not null.
    (HAS(drug.fda_appr_dt) == 1) &

    # Condition 3: The treatment has a recorded end date.
    # HAS(end_dt) == 1 checks if the end_dt field for the treatment is not null.
    (HAS(end_dt) == 1) &

    # Condition 4: The treatment's end date is within the last 6 months from today.
    # This means the end_dt must be on or after six_months_ago and on or before current_date.
    (end_dt >= six_months_ago) &
    (end_dt <= current_date)
)

# Count the number of treatments that satisfy all the above conditions.
# The result is calculated on the top-level DermTreatment collection.
result = DermTreatment.CALCULATE(
    num_matching_treatments = COUNT(filtered_treatments)
)

# Get all successful transactions for each merchant (both sent and received)
merchant_transaction_data = Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=name,
    # Count distinct successful transactions (both sent and received)
    total_successful_transactions=NDISTINCT(
        transactions_sent.WHERE(status == "SUCCESS").txid
    ) + NDISTINCT(
        transactions_received.WHERE(status == "SUCCESS").txid
    ),
    # Count distinct coupons used in successful transactions (both sent and received)
    distinct_coupons_used=NDISTINCT(
        transactions_sent.WHERE((status == "SUCCESS") & (coupon_id != None)).coupon_id
    ) + NDISTINCT(
        transactions_received.WHERE((status == "SUCCESS") & (coupon_id != None)).coupon_id
    )
).WHERE(total_successful_transactions > 0)

# Calculate CPUR for each merchant
result = merchant_transaction_data.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    cpur=distinct_coupons_used / total_successful_transactions
)

# Step 1: Calculate intermediate counts for successful transactions per merchant.
# We access Merchants through the Ewallet graph.
# For each merchant, we filter their received transactions for those with status 'completed'.
# Then, we count the number of distinct coupon IDs used in these successful transactions
# and the total number of distinct successful transactions.
merchant_stats = Ewallet.Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name_val = name,
    num_distinct_coupons_val = NDISTINCT(transactions_received.WHERE(status == 'completed').coupon_id),
    num_distinct_txs_val = COUNT(transactions_received.WHERE(status == 'completed').txid)
)

# Step 2: Calculate CPUR using the counts from Step 1 and select the final fields.
# We use the IFF function to handle cases where a merchant might have zero successful transactions,
# setting CPUR to 0.0 in such cases to prevent division by zero.
# We ensure floating-point division by multiplying the numerator by 1.0.
result = merchant_stats.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name = merchant_name_val,
    CPUR = IFF(
        num_distinct_txs_val > 0,
        (num_distinct_coupons_val * 1.0) / num_distinct_txs_val,
        0.0
    )
)

# Filter notifications for US and Canada users from last 3 weeks (excluding current week)
us_canada_notifications = Users.WHERE(
    ISIN(country, ("US", "Canada"))
).notifications.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("weeks", created_at, DATETIME('now')) >= 1 &
    DATEDIFF("weeks", created_at, DATETIME('now')) <= 3
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(created_at), (1, 7))  # Saturday=7, Sunday=1
)

# Group by week and calculate metrics
result = us_canada_notifications.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_notifications=COUNT(Notifications),
    weekend_notifications=SUM(Notifications.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

    (created_at >= three_weeks_ago_start_ts) & (created_at < current_week_start_ts)
)

# Calculate the week of the notification and if it was on a weekend
notifications_with_details = relevant_notifications.CALCULATE(
    notification_week_val = DATETIME(created_at, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend_notification = ISIN(DAYNAME(created_at), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# Partition by the notification week
weekly_notification_groups = notifications_with_details.PARTITION(
    name="notifications_per_week", by=(notification_week_val)
)

# Calculate total and weekend notifications for each week
weekly_summary = weekly_notification_groups.CALCULATE(
    week_start = notification_week_val,
    total_notifications = COUNT(Notifications),
    weekend_notifications = SUM(IFF(is_weekend_notification, 1, 0))
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

result = weekly_summary

    (sender_type == 0) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", created_at, DATETIME('now')) >= 1) &

[DEBUG] [Q102] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the current month and the start of the period two months ago.
# For example, if today is July 15th:
# - current_month_start_dt will be July 1st, 00:00:00.
# - two_months_ago_start_dt will be May 1st, 00:00:00.
# The filter will include transactions from May 1st up to (but not including) July 1st.
current_month_start_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
two_months_ago_start_dt = DATETIME(current_month_start_dt, '-2 months')

# 1. Filter transactions for sender_type = 0 and within the last two full months.
#    Also, prepare data for daily aggregation by extracting day and month from created_at.
transactions_for_dau_calculation = Ewallet.Transactions.WHERE(
    (sender_type == 0) &
    (created_at >= two_months_ago_start_dt) &
    (created_at < current_month_start_dt)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_day_ts = DATETIME(created_at, 'start of day'),     # Timestamp for the specific day of transaction
    transaction_month_ts = DATETIME(created_at, 'start of month'), # Timestamp for the month of transaction
    user_sender_id = sender_id                                     # Sender ID for distinct counting
)

# 2. Calculate Daily Active Users (DAU).
#    Group by the specific day (transaction_day_ts) and the month it belongs to (transaction_month_ts).
#    For each day, count the number of distinct senders.
dau_by_day_calculation = transactions_for_dau_calculation.PARTITION(
    name="daily_active_users_group", by=(transaction_day_ts, transaction_month_ts)
).CALCULATE(
    day_key = transaction_day_ts,          # The day for which DAU is calculated
    month_key = transaction_month_ts,      # The month this day belongs to (carried for next step)
    daily_active_count = NDISTINCT(transactions_for_dau_calculation.user_sender_id) # Distinct active users for this day
)

# 3. Calculate Per Month Daily Active Users (PMDAU).
#    Group the daily DAU counts by month (month_key).
#    For each month, calculate the average of the daily_active_count.
pmdau_final_calculation = dau_by_day_calculation.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_pmdau_group", by=(month_key)
).CALCULATE(
    result_month = month_key,  # The month for which PMDAU is reported
    pmdau = AVG(dau_by_day_calculation.daily_active_count) # Average of DAUs for that month
).ORDER_BY(result_month.ASC()) # Order results by month ascending

result = pmdau_final_calculation

# Filter transactions from the previous month
previous_month_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (YEAR(created_at) == YEAR(DATETIME('now', '-1 month'))) &
    (MONTH(created_at) == MONTH(DATETIME('now', '-1 month')))
)

# Calculate STR for the previous month

    total_transactions=COUNT(previous_month_transactions),
    successful_transactions=COUNT(previous_month_transactions.WHERE(status == 'success')),
    str_rate=100.0 * COUNT(previous_month_transactions.WHERE(status == 'success')) / COUNT(previous_month_transactions)
)

previous_month_str = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    # Determine the first day of the previous month to establish the target year and month
    _first_day_prev_month = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 month')
).CALCULATE(
    # Extract year and month for filtering transactions
    _target_year = YEAR(_first_day_prev_month),
    _target_month = MONTH(_first_day_prev_month)
).CALCULATE(
    # Count successful transactions in the previous month
    _num_successful_transactions = COUNT(
        Transactions.WHERE(
            (YEAR(created_at) == _target_year) &
            (MONTH(created_at) == _target_month) &
            (status == 'success')
        )
    ),
    # Count total transactions in the previous month
    _num_total_transactions = COUNT(
        Transactions.WHERE(
            (YEAR(created_at) == _target_year) &
            (MONTH(created_at) == _target_month)
        )
    )
).CALCULATE(
    # Calculate STR as a percentage, handling division by zero
    str_value_previous_month = IFF(
        _num_total_transactions > 0,
        100.0 * _num_successful_transactions / _num_total_transactions,
        0.0  # STR is 0% if there were no transactions in the previous month
    )
)

# First, get merchant info with their creation month
merchant_info = Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_id=mid,
    merchant_name=name,
    merchant_creation_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(created_at), MONTH(created_at))
)

# Get coupon info with their creation month and link to merchant
coupon_info = merchant_info.coupons.CALCULATE(
    coupon_creation_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(created_at), MONTH(created_at))
)

# Filter coupons that were created in the same month as their merchant
same_month_coupons = coupon_info.WHERE(
    coupon_creation_month == merchant_creation_month
)

# Count coupons per merchant for the same creation month
merchant_coupon_counts = merchant_info.CALCULATE(
    merchant_id=merchant_id,
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    num_coupons=COUNT(same_month_coupons)
)

# Find the merchant with the highest number of coupons
result = merchant_coupon_counts.TOP_K(1, by=num_coupons.DESC())

# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only those from 2023, completed, sent by an active user.
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (YEAR(created_at) == 2023) &  # Transaction occurred in 2023
    (status == 'completed') &  # Transaction was successful
    (HAS(sending_user) == 1) &  # Sender is a user (not a merchant)
    (sending_user.status == 'active')  # Sending user is active
)

# Step 2: For the filtered transactions, calculate the transaction month (as the first day of the month)
# and get the UID of the sending user.
transactions_info = filtered_transactions.CALCULATE(
    transaction_month_date = DATETIME(created_at, 'start of month'),  # Get the first day of the transaction month
    user_sender_uid = sending_user.uid  # Get the UID of the active sending user
)

# Step 3: Group by the transaction month date and count the number of distinct active users (senders).
monthly_distinct_active_senders = transactions_info.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_user_transactions", by=(transaction_month_date)
).CALCULATE(
    month = transaction_month_date,  # The month, represented as a date (first day of the month)
    num_distinct_active_users = NDISTINCT(user_sender_uid)  # Count of distinct active users who sent money
)

# Step 4: Order the results by month in ascending order (earliest month first).
result = monthly_distinct_active_senders.ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Step 1 & 2: Filter transactions and calculate necessary fields for grouping and aggregation.
# We get the sender's country via the 'sending_user' relationship,
# the transaction amount, and the sender's ID.
user_transactions_info = Transactions.WHERE(sender_type == 0).CALCULATE(
    user_country = sending_user.country,
    tx_amount = amount,
    original_sender_id = sender_id  # 'sender_id' from Transactions is the user's ID
)

# Step 3 & 4: Partition by country and calculate the required aggregates.
# For each country, we count distinct senders and sum their transaction amounts.
country_summary_metrics = user_transactions_info.PARTITION(
    name="country_groups", by=(user_country)
).CALCULATE(
    country = user_country,
    number_of_distinct_users_who_sent = NDISTINCT(Transactions.original_sender_id),
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(Transactions.tx_amount)
)

# Step 5: Select the top 5 countries by total transaction amount.
top_5_countries_by_sent_amount = country_summary_metrics.TOP_K(
    5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC()
)

result = top_5_countries_by_sent_amount

    # Calculate the duration of each session in seconds
